the company · components/ad-variations · internal only — not for sale

Ad Variations

One ad that already worked goes in. Out comes the plan: every element marked keep or test, with the brand's own numbers behind it; one-lever variations, highest impact first; new formats built from parts the brand has already proven; which seconds of the original get reused, what AI makes, what it costs, and what each one must pass. It plans — nothing is generated here.

How it worksTest runsVariable IndexPromptsAgainst the hand-made specHow to run it

How it works

Each stage is its own prompt, so each can be read and tightened on its own. Numbers never come from a model: the index counts them, and the machine prices every recipe from the route list.

  1. indexVariable IndexEvery teardown the brand has, countedrule reads + a Sonnet coder per post, cached
  2. 0MeasureThe file's own clock: cuts, loudness, fpsffmpeg — no model, $0
  3. 1Anatomy → elementsThe ad by the second, on nineteen fixed elementsClaude Opus
  4. 2Locks vs leversKeep or test each element, ranked by estimated impactClaude Opus · numbers from the index
  5. 3Variation matrixN variations, one lever each, highest impact firstClaude Opus
  6. 4New formatsProven parts recombined into formats not yet runClaude Opus
  7. 5Merge recipeSeconds reused, what AI makes, route — the machine prices itClaude Opus · $ from routes.json
  8. 6QC gatesThe gates each variation must passClaude Opus · forced gates from routes
  9. 7CongruenceThe static and page section each variation feedsClaude Opus
Input

A finished video teardown of a post (triage, record + verbatim transcript, doctrine read, audience read, replication spec), the post's views and saves, and the brand folder.

Output

variation-plan.json — the plan; no component reads it yet — plus a Markdown copy and this page.

Planning cost

≈$2.38 per ad (Claude Opus, seven stages); the brand's Variable Index costs ≈$2 once for 60 posts and then only codes new posts.

Routes it may plan on

GPT Image direct via OpenAI · Seedance on Higgsfield · Genjutsu motion transfer (Wan 2.2 Animate and Kling Motion Control flagged TEST) · the local studio for cut-outs, tracking, interpolation, stems, captions · ElevenLabs voice.

Runs on this page

Variations

V00
Control — the original rebuilt for paid: 4:5 safe zone, captions, one offer, clean plate
$0.00 · ≈0.5 h
  • V-hook.05 First-frame composition: picture-in-picture with the reactor bottom-right of a 9:16 frame → picture-in-picture rebuilt inside the 4:5 crop — reactor moved up out of the bottom cut zone, borrowed clip re-scaled so the device stays in the safe area
  • V-text.04 On-screen text position: top → upper-third inside the 4:5 safe zone — the comment-reply sticker re-laid so it is not cropped off the top
  • V-text.01 Running captions: none → burned-in captions on all nine spoken lines, same words, no rewording
  • V-ask.05 Price or offer stated: none → one offer at the close — NOVA $69.99 with the 90-day refund guarantee, one button
  • V-ask.06 End frame: product-to-lens, framed for 9:16, no card → product-to-lens re-framed for 4:5, holding two extra beats for the offer line and the disclaimer “90-day refund guarantee. Terms apply.”

INFERENCE — the original loses nothing that made it work when it is rebuilt for paid: the same hook, the same silent demo, the same close, only made legible muted and inside the crop. Every V01–V10 result is read against this, not against the organic 16.4M post.

Confirms if: V00 holds a 3-second hold rate and save rate in the same band as the organic original, so the rebuild is a clean plate and the later variations are measuring their own lever. · Kills if: V00 collapses against the organic original on hold rate — then the rebuild itself broke something (most likely the PIP re-layout or the captions covering the sticker) and no variation beneath it can be read.

Aimed at: fed-up-king none · paid cut

Needs a ruling: Which single offer the paid cut carries. position.md fixes it at one price, one offer, one button and the survey named option-stacking as the deal breaker — this plan assumes NOVA at $69.99 plus the compliance.json guarantee line, and that assumption is the owner's to confirm or replace.

  • 0.00–21.70 The whole original cut, frame for frame — hook PIP, aisle flash, shelf lift and case open, both use blocks, the reveal and the close. Nothing is regenerated: the paid plate is a re-layout and a text pass over footage already shot.

Where the record and the file differ

  • The record says the music is none — the Music line reads “Music: none”, every shot row reads “music: none”, and the sound sheet reads “No added sound effects; all audio is room tone/production audio” ; the file shows the isolated non-voice stem at or above −45 dB in fourteen half-second windows — 7.0, 7.5, 8.0, 8.5, 9.0, 9.5, 10.0, 10.5, 14.5, 15.0, 16.0, 16.5, 17.0, 17.5 and 18.0 s — peaking at −16 dB at 15.0 s and hitting −27 dB at 14.5 s immediately after four consecutive near-silent windows. On the measurement there is a bed under the acquisition montage, a bed under the disgust attack, and two isolated hits on the reveal and the turn.
  • The record's own opening block says the music is an aggressive drift-phonk track with a drop cutting in at 0:07, stopping at 0:11 and slamming back at 0:14, referencing Kordhell and Interworld ; the same record's Music line and every shot row say music: none. The record contradicts itself. The file shows the stem entering at 7.0 s, dropping out after 10.5 s and returning at 14.5 s — which matches the phonk description's 0:07 / 0:11 / 0:14 almost exactly and contradicts the none line.
  • The record says the shot table has 13 rows and the index gives V-edit.03 shot count 13, average shot 1.69 s ; the file shows 10 hard cuts (scene score ≥ 25) and an average shot of 2.17 s, with four soft changes at 4.09 s (16), 4.42 s (14), 10.14 s (20) and 20.35 s (14) that do not reach cut threshold.
  • The record says the hook is four separate rows — 0:00.0–0:01.5, 0:01.5–0:03.0, 0:03.0–0:05.0, 0:05.0–0:06.5 ; the file shows one cut at 0.46 s and then a single uncut 4.21 s shot from 2.13 s to 6.34 s, so the record's 3.0 s and 5.0 s row boundaries have no cut behind them.
  • The record says the hook block ends and the store begins at 0:06.5 ; the file shows the cut at 6.34 s (score 42) — a 0.16 s difference, inside tolerance, but the file also shows a second cut at 6.97 s (score 54) splitting the record's single 0:06.5–0:08.5 store row in two, which the record does not have.
  • The record says the case-opening beat runs 0:08.5–0:09.5 as its own row ; the file shows no cut at 8.5 s — the measured shot runs 6.97 s to 9.93 s unbroken, merging the shelf lift and the case open into one continuous action.
  • The record says the transformation beat starts at 0:14.0 with a jump-cut to a suddenly groomed face ; the file shows no cut anywhere between 11.64 s and 14.89 s — the measured shot spans 3.25 s straight through 14.0 s. What the file does show at that moment is an audio event: mix −55 dB at 14.0 s jumping to −27 dB at 14.5 s with the music stem jumping −76 dB to −27 dB at the same instant.

the owner decides

  • Can we stitch a creator's video we do not own? The rank-1 lever is the borrowed clip behind the picture-in-picture (V-hook.01 g: 15 posts, save rate 0.88% vs 0.55%), and identity-anchors.md §3 settles cutting footage the brand OWNS while §3b stops the chain on generating a creator's likeness — a stitch is neither. Every variation of this ad's hook needs this answered first.
  • Which public figures may be named as the result benchmark? V-hook.09 named: 10 posts, 33.9% of views, save rate 0.95% vs 0.56% — naming is the stronger side in our own corpus, and position.md already carries a customer saying “I wanted to be like ASAP Rocky.” Nothing in the NEVER list or compliance.json covers a third-party name beside a skin result.
  • Which single offer does the paid cut carry? V-ask.05 shows 51 posts and 100% of top-quartile views with no offer stated — so the corpus has never tried one, and a paid placement has to. position.md's binding rule is one price, one offer, one button, and the survey named option-stacking as the deal breaker, so this is a ruling, not a maker's pick.
  • Do we move off peer-to-peer to real-person? V-tal.06 peer-to-peer is 45 posts and 78.6% of views but saves 0.63% against the rest's 0.93%, and ALL FIVE near-twins run real-person instead, including rank 3 at 9,400,000 views. story.md's VOICE line fixes the barbershop register and bars the sales register — it does not say which of these two reads that is. Cost class is reshoot.
  • Is the market stage confirmed? position.md's block says `confirmed by: open` and the Open section calls stage 4 declared, not confirmed. The awareness-at-entry lever (V-struct.04 solution-aware: 14 posts, save rate 0.82% vs 0.58%) rests on it, and the corpus reads this audience one stage back at problem-aware (V-aud.06: 31 posts, 60.6% of views).
  • Does the signature asset get built? position.md lists SIGNATURE ASSET as open — the neck, same man, same wall, same light, day 1 and day 90, and says it does not exist yet. The result-shot lever cannot move from exaggerated-comedic (4 posts, save rate 0.81% vs 0.65%) to a literal result — which rank 3 at 9,400,000 views runs — without it, and a generated before-and-after is a claim, not a likeness question.

F05
Nothing For Nine Seconds
$0.40

NEW — untested

Silent b-roll of the shave itself opens the piece — no voice, no text, just the razor and the skin — and the first words land only after the viewer has already watched the problem happen.

  • V-hook.01 b-silent — 4 posts, 2.6% of views, save rate 0.67%
  • V-hook.02 1–2 — 3 posts, 13.1% of views, save rate 1.17%
  • V-edit.05 3–7 — 9 posts, 18.8% of views, save rate 0.96%
  • V-text.02 0–1 — 6 posts, 20.0% of views, save rate 0.83%
  • V-struct.02 story / testimonial — 8 posts, 17.0% of views, save rate 1.25%
  • V-proof.02 felt-not-shown — 7 posts, 9.9% of views, save rate 0.99%
  • V-sound.10 12+ — 6 posts, 4.2% of views, save rate 1.15%
  • V-edit.02 46 s + — 7 posts, 6.0% of views, save rate 1.76%
  • V-sound.02 music bed — 32 posts, 28.4% of views, save rate 0.88%

INFERENCE — this is the brand's save format, built deliberately against its view format. Every part here is a low-view, high-save value: 46 s + runtime saves at 1.76%, the highest single number in the index, on only 6.0% of views; story / testimonial framework 1.25%; first spoken word at 1–2 s 1.17%; 12+ spoken lines 1.15%; three-to-seven seconds of silent b-roll 0.96%; felt-not-shown results 0.99%. Every one of them is where the brand's saves already concentrate and where it almost never ships. Holding the first word to 1–2 s inverts the source ad's 0.36 s open, and the story block's SPINE — he hid it, he found out it was never his fault, he shows his face again — needs length and quiet that a 19-second step list cannot carry. A save is a man coming back to it later, which is the behaviour the-hider story actually asks for.

Closest existing: story-open × mechanism-explained × testimonial-montage (1 post, 2.6% of views) and story-open × live-demo × micro-drama (1 post, 0.9%). Both open on a told story; neither opens silent, and no run combination carries a silent b-roll open at all.

Seed footage: The corpus's nine posts with three-to-seven seconds of silent b-roll are the pacing reference. Sonya (29 clips · 123 min) and Mia (28 clips · 89 min) carry the long-form spoken register a 46 s + runtime needs, and the asset index's 1,342 clips are the source for shave b-roll. The source ad contributes only one thing, and it is the key one: its 15.0–16.5 s near-silence before the verdict — four consecutive readings at −49/−49/−49/−51 dB — is the brand's proof that dropping everything out before a line works, extended here from 1.5 seconds to nine. · To make: The silent open, which the source ad has none of — its V-edit.05 is 0 seconds and every second is either speech or the action's own sound. A music bed, which the source has none of. A twelve-plus-line told story rather than a six-step list. The whole 46 s + structure, which sits outside the brand's dominant 16–24 s band. Constraint: felt-not-shown means no result claim on screen, so this one carries no before-after and no disclaimer beyond the testimonial line if a real man tells it.

Where the record and the file differ

  • The record's shot table has 10 rows; the file measures 7 hard cuts (scene-score ≥ 25) plus 4 soft changes (4.70 s score 17, 6.00 s score 21, 7.00 s score 22, 17.30 s score 14). The index's V-edit.03 (shot count) carries 10 and V-edit.04 (average shot) carries 1.8 s, which are the record's rows; the file gives 7 shots at an average of 2.72 s.
  • The record's roll sheet totals 16.5 s of A plus 1.5 s of B-over = 18.0 s; the file is 19.04 s. Roughly one second of measured runtime sits past the end of the record's last row (0:18.000).
  • The record ends its final row at 0:18.000 and its transcript places T7 at 0:17.3; the file's last audio reading is at 19.0 s (−58 dB) with non-voice energy at 18.5 s (−36 dB). The record does not account for the 18.0–19.04 s tail.
  • The record has four separate rows between 0:02.500 and 0:09.200 (problem, lick, exfoliate-line, brush-start); the file measures no hard cut in that span at all — 3.40–9.20 is one continuous 5.80 s shot, the longest in the file. The record's internal boundaries at 4.5 and 6.5 correspond to soft changes at 4.70 (17) and 6.00/7.00 (21/22), below the hard-cut threshold.
  • The record puts the brush-scrub row at 0:08.500–0:11.500; the file measures that shot at 9.20–12.30 — both ends off by ~0.7–0.8 s, more than the 0.3 s tolerance.
  • The record puts the peel row at 0:11.500–0:13.500; the file measures 12.30–13.87 — the head off by 0.8 s.
  • The record puts the balm-application row at 0:13.500–0:14.500 and a second row “*apply*” at 0:14.500–0:16.500; the file measures one shot at 13.87–15.13 with the next cut at 15.13, so the record's 14.5 caption boundary falls inside a single measured shot and its 16.5 boundary has no measured cut at all (17.30 s carries only a soft change, score 14).
  • The record puts the hook row at 0:00.000–0:01.000 and the brush row starting at 0:01.000; the file's first measured cut is at 1.93 s, which matches the transcript's T2 at 0:01.9 rather than the record's 1.0 s row boundary. V-prod.01 (product first on screen) carries 1.0 from the record and cannot be confirmed before 1.93 by the measurement.
  • The record says “Music: none” and “Sound form: SPOKEN for the entire runtime”, and V-sound.02 (music bed) carries none; the isolated music stem crosses −45 dB at fifteen half-second marks — 1.0, 1.5, 3.5, 4.0, 5.0, 8.5, 9.0, 9.5, 10.0, 10.5, 11.0, 11.5, 12.0, 12.5, 14.0 and 18.5 s. Most sit on actions the record names, but the readings at 1.0–1.5 s and 18.5 s are named nowhere in the record's sound sheet.
  • The record's sound sheet lists exactly two non-speech events (0:04.500 wet smack, 0:08.500 brush buzz) and V-sound.11 (added sound effects) carries none; the stem shows five distinct non-voice runs, including the 14.0 s reading (−20 dB, the record's finger-friction, which appears in the shot table but not the sound sheet) and the 18.5 s reading.
  • The record's cast card gives the creator's age as [20-30]; the voice card on the same record gives 18-24, and V-tal.04 (lead voice age band) carries 18-24. The record contradicts itself.
  • The record's shot table row at 0:14.500–0:16.500 gives the transcript as [silence]; the transcript table places T6 at 0:13.9 and T7 at 0:17.3, which is consistent — but the measured mix shows −15 dB at 14.0 s, the third-loudest window in the file, sitting inside a row the record calls silent.

the owner decides

  • Can a variation put a different face on this performance? The face is frame one and it is a single-post value in the corpus (V-tal.09: 1 post, 13.9% of views, save rate 0.41% vs 0.74%), so it is the biggest untested thing in the ad — but identity-anchors.md §3b leaves generating new performance from a retained creator's face OPEN and says a run that needs it should stop. The thirteen roster men are clear to generate (§1); this creator is not.
  • Does the hook line get rewritten onto the brand's own problem? This ad's hook is “How to get rid of chapped lips.” and its steps say exfoliate and dead skin — position.md's NEVER list bans exfoliation / dead skin cells, its PROBLEM WORD is bumps · ingrown hair · “my neck feels like braille”, and V-aud.04's three lock-leaning topics are razor-bumps (40 posts, 78.0% of saves, 0.74% vs 0.56%), ingrown-hairs and shaving. Chapped lips is not one of them, yet this is the brand's #2 post at 12,400,000 views.
  • Is the plain claim allowed here? position.md → LEADS WITH says “never the plain claim”, and V-struct.06 reads this ad's signature as exactly that (10 posts, 33.5% of views, save rate 0.63% vs 0.72%). Either the variations move off it, or the ruling gets an exception for this format.
  • May a variation name a competitor or a public figure? V-hook.09 “not named” covers 50 posts at a 0.56% save rate against 0.95% for the ten that do name one — the widest hook gap in the table. position.md names Bevel, Tend Skin, PFB Vanish and Bump Patrol, and the bank carries “I wanted to be like ASAP Rocky”, but naming one on a live asset is not ruled anywhere.
  • Do the paid variations get an offer written now, or wait? A paid cut has to carry one, and V-ask.05 shows 100.0% of top-quartile views on “no price stated” — the organic corpus says never, the paid cut says always. position.md fixes the shape (one price, one offer, one button) but not whether these variations carry it.
  • Does the multi-place / multi-body direction get tested against the close? V-look.02 (0.33% vs 0.85%), V-tal.01 (0.34% vs 0.83%) and V-proof.04 (0.36% vs 0.89%) all read lock-leaning → 3+, pointing away from this ad's one-body one-place build — but stage 1 marks the same-wall close as what makes the result readable. Adding places means rebuilding the close.
  • Which shot-count figure the variations are cut to: the record's 10 shots at 1.8 s or the file's measured 7 at 2.72 s. V-edit.04 puts 78.3% of top-quartile views on a 1.5–2.5 s average, and the measurement says this ad is at 2.72 s — outside the band the corpus converges on. The evidence table and the file disagree about what this ad does.

Anatomy → elementsprompts/stage-1-elements/stage1-elements-v3-owner.md · 696 words
You are stage 1 of the ad-variations chain: ANATOMY → ELEMENTS. One proven ad
has already been torn down. Your job is to map it onto a fixed list of
elements, by the second, so every ad from any brand lands in the same shape.
You describe; you do not recommend. Every example in this prompt is
illustrative and names no real brand or product.

THE AD'S METRICS
{metrics}

THE TEARDOWN RECORD (objective record, shot table, verbatim transcript, voices)
{teardown_record}

THE REPLICATION SPEC (the reusable construct)
{replication_spec}

THE DOCTRINE READ
{doctrine_read}

THE AUDIENCE READ
{audience_read}

THE FILE'S OWN CLOCK (stage 0 — measured with ffmpeg and a music stem; no model)
{measurement}

You cannot see the video. The measurement gives you WHERE the cuts are and HOW
LOUD each half-second is; the record gives you WHAT is on screen. So:
- A measured cut that falls inside one record row splits that row's picture;
  a measured shot that spans several record rows merges them. Never move a
  picture across a measured cut the record's own times do not support — when
  you cannot tell which side of a cut a picture belongs to, say so in `why`
  and mark the beat's picture "from the record, placement uncertain".
- Sound comes from the measurement first. The music stem is the truth about
  music: where it is at or above −45 dB there is a bed or a hit, whatever the
  record's Music line says. Name the stem's loudest window and any window
  where the stem hits while the voice is silent — that is usually a planned
  sound hit on a reveal.

THIS AD'S VALUES IN THE VARIABLE INDEX (the shared vocabulary — id · name · value)
{ad_values}

THE FIXED ELEMENT LIST (key · name · the index variables it covers)
{element_list}

Do three things.

1. BEATS. Walk the ad beat by beat on the MEASURED shots when they exist (the
   record's rows when they do not). One beat per measured shot; merge only
   shots that are one continuous action. For each: start and end in seconds
   (two decimals, the file's clock), the section, what is on screen, the words
   (T-number and verbatim), the sound (mix and music stem — name the loudest
   and the quietest stretches, and where the music enters, drops and hits),
   and the share of the runtime the beat starts at, and an attention call: WON (the first
   beat that stops the scroll), HELD, RE-WON (a new cut, place, sound or
   register that re-earns attention), or CLOSE.

2. ELEMENTS. Fill every key of the fixed list — all nineteen, no more, no
   fewer. For each: what this ad does, in plain words, with the seconds; the
   variable ids it covers with this ad's value for each (copy the index value;
   if the index value is wrong for this ad — the measurement contradicts it,
   or the record contradicts itself — give the right one and say why in
   `note`); and `source`: record, measured, or both. Where an element is made
   of parts production could change separately (who is on screen, the words
   on a sticker, the clip behind a picture-in-picture, a public figure named
   in a line), list each part in `parts` with its own seconds.

3. RECORD VS FILE. List every place where the teardown record and the
   measurement disagree (a cut the record does not have, a music line the
   loudness contradicts, a speaker the voice card contradicts, a timestamp off
   by more than 0.3 s). State both sides; do not resolve them.

The json must parse. Inside any string value write quotation marks as curly
quotes (“ ”) or escape them (\"); a straight double quote inside a value
breaks the whole block.

Return one fenced json block and nothing else:

  "beats": [
    {"n": 1, "start": 0.0, "end": 0.0, "section": "", "picture": "", "words": "", "sound": "", "attention": "WON | HELD | RE-WON | CLOSE", "why": ""}

Locks vs leversprompts/stage-2-locks/stage2-locks-v3-owner.md · 735 words
You are stage 2 of the ad-variations chain: LOCKS VS LEVERS. Every element of
one proven ad is on the table below. For each one, say whether it is a proven
LOCK (keep it exactly) or a LEVER (a variation may move it), and rank the
levers by estimated impact. Every example here is illustrative.

THE AD'S METRICS
{metrics}

THE ELEMENTS (stage 1)
{elements}

THE EVIDENCE — computed by the machine from the brand's whole teardown corpus
(the Variable Index), not by a model. For each variable: this ad's value, how
many of the brand's posts share it, the share of all views and saves those
posts hold, their save rate against the rest, and the index's lock/lever
tendency.
{evidence}

THE CLOSEST POSTS IN THE BRAND'S OWN CORPUS — computed: the posts that share
the most of what a viewer sees and hears with this ad, their views, and every
variable they differ on. A near-twin that differs on one or two variables is
the brand's own half-run test of those variables: if its views differ a lot,
those variables have been seen to move results.
{siblings}

THE BRAND'S RULES (position, story, likeness, compliance)
{brand_rules}

Call the PARTS, not only the elements. Where stage 1 split an element into
parts (who reacts, the sticker's words, the clip behind a picture-in-picture,
a named public figure), give each part its own row: the layout can be a lock
while the person in it is a lever.

Call it twice when the cuts differ. The brand's corpus is organic posts. A
paid cut plays muted by default, is cropped to 4:5, and has to carry an
offer; an element that is a lock for the organic cut can be a lever for the
paid cut (captions, the end frame, the ask, a subject outside the 4:5 zone).
Say which cut each call is for: `cut` = both, organic or paid.

The element key is always one of the nineteen keys in the elements above.
A call about order, awareness or framing goes under the nearest key
(problem_line, ask_placement, camouflage) with the part named.

How to call it:
- LOCK when the corpus shows the brand's top posts converge on this ad's value
  (tendency lock-leaning) AND no near-twin moved results by changing it, or
  when changing it would break what the replication spec marks load-bearing,
  or when a brand rule fixes it.
- LEVER when the top posts vary on it; when a near-twin differs on it and its
  views differ (quote the twin's rank and views); when this ad's value
  conflicts with the audience read or a brand rule; or when the paid cut
  needs it moved. A value the whole corpus shares is not evidence that it
  works — only that nothing else was tried; say so rather than calling it a
  lock on count alone.
- Evidence is quoted ONLY from the evidence table and the records above —
  numbers exactly as given, with the variable id. Never invent a number.
- Rank the ten levers with the highest estimated impact, 1 to 10. Every
  other lever keeps `impact_rank` null and `impact` "later" — it is still a
  lever, just not in the first set.
- Nothing in the corpus isolates one variable at a time. The impact rank is
  therefore an ESTIMATE, and says what it weighs: how much of the first three
  seconds the element controls, whether the brand's posts show it moving
  results, and what it costs to test (cost class from the evidence table).
- If a lever needs a ruling before it can move (a real person's likeness, a
  voice clone, a public figure, a claim), name the ruling in `needs_ruling`.
  Do not decide it.

The json must parse. Inside any string value write quotation marks as curly
quotes (“ ”) or escape them (\"); a straight double quote inside a value
breaks the whole block.

Return one fenced json block and nothing else:

```json
{
  "calls": [
    {"element": "hook_visual", "part": "who reacts | null", "cut": "both | organic | paid", "call": "LOCK | LEVER", "variables": ["V-hook.07"], "what_this_ad_does": "",
     "evidence": "V-hook.07 comment-reply: 17 posts, 41.3% of views, save rate 0.85% vs …",
     "needs_ruling": null, "impact_rank": null, "impact": "high | medium | low | null",
     "impact_basis": "ESTIMATE — …"}
  ],
  "lever_order": ["element keys, highest estimated impact first"],
  "owner_decides": ["a question only the owner can answer, with the fact that raises it"]
}
```

proven ad's locks and levers, plan {n} variations. Each changes ONE lever — at
most two index variables, and two only when they cannot move apart — so every

here changes the ad's format, framework or structure. Every example here is illustrative.

THE AD'S METRICS
{metrics}

THE ELEMENTS (stage 1)
{elements}

THE LOCKS AND LEVERS (stage 2), with the lever order
{calls}

THE CLOSEST POSTS IN THE BRAND'S CORPUS (computed)
{siblings}

THE AUDIENCE READ (who the ad speaks to, and how our audience differs)
{audience_read}

{avatars}

THE CUSTOMER'S OWN WORDS (any new line, sticker or caption is taken from here
verbatim, with its id)
{language}

THE CAST WE MAY USE (synthetic, cleared) 
{cast}

{products}

THE BRAND'S RULES
{brand_rules}

Rules:
- Start with V00, the CONTROL: the original rebuilt only as much as paid
  placement needs (safe zones, a clean plate). Every variation is read against
  it. If the original needs no rebuild, V00 is the original as-is.
- Then V01…V{n}, highest estimated impact first, following the lever order —
  cheaper tests of an equal lever go first. Cover the levers, not one lever
  many ways: no two variations move the same variable unless they aim it at
  different sub-avatars (two different cast members for the same person-lever
  is the one common case).

  the 4:5 zone, one offer and one button at the end, the hero SKU. Each need
  the original does not meet is a lever and gets its own variation.

  shows only one, one variation tests the ad on another hero, and says which
  lines stay true.
- A person-lever (who reacts, who pitches, who demonstrates) is cast from the
  cleared cast list above by name, matched to a sub-avatar. A real
  creator's or customer's likeness is never regenerated — it goes in
  `needs_ruling`.
- Each change is written against the Variable Index: variable id, from (this
  ad's value), to (the new value). A change with no variable id is not
  allowed; if the lever has no index variable, use the closest id and say so.
- New words are verbatim from the customer's words above, with their ids. If
  nothing there fits, write [BANK LINE NEEDED: what it must say] — never
  invent customer language.
- A variation that needs a ruling (a real person's likeness, a cloned voice, a
  public figure, a retail claim) says so in `needs_ruling` and still gets
  planned; the ruling is the owner's.
- Name the avatar and sub-avatar each variation is aimed at, and whether it is
  a paid cut, an organic cut, or both.
- Hypotheses are INFERENCE: say what result would confirm or kill each one.

quotes (“ ”) or escape them (\"); a straight double quote inside a value
breaks the whole block.

Hand-made spec: lab/owner/-relaunch/ASSET-01-VIDEO-VARIATIONS-owner.md (page (private page))

How to run it on any ad

Say it

“Run ad variations on post 7185208483388689706.”

Any post that has been torn down works — a post id, or the name of its teardown run. Add what you want: “twelve variations”, “only paid cuts”, “use the relaunch plan for the page sections”.

What the session does

  1. Checks the post has a finished teardown (triage, record, doctrine, audience, spec). If it doesn't, it runs the teardown first.
  2. Refreshes the brand's Variable Index — only posts torn down since the last build get coded, so it takes seconds when nothing is new.
  3. Runs a free dry run: every prompt filled, nothing spent, the planning cost estimated.
  4. Runs the seven stages, then adds the run to this page and republishes it — same link.

What comes back

  • A new set of cards on the Test runs tab: locks and levers, the variations, new formats, costs, gates.
  • variations.json in the run folder — the file the production machine reads next.
  • A Markdown copy for the team beside it.

Where it stops and asks you

  • Anything under “the owner decides” — likeness, voice cloning, public figures, the hero SKU, the offer.
  • A variation marked “Needs a ruling” is planned but never produced until you rule.
